(Figure right) is a new framework to train an arbitrary LM to learn to retrieve, generate, and critique to enhance the factuality and quality of generations, without hurting the versatility of LLMs.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eUnlike a widely-adopted Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG; Figure left) approach, \u003cstrong\u003eSelf-RAG\u003c/strong\u003e retrieves on demand (e.g., can retrieve multiple times or completely skip retrieval) given diverse queries, and criticize its own generation from multiple fine-grained aspects by predicting \u003cstrong\u003ereflection tokens\u003c/strong\u003e as an integral part of generation.\nWe conduct a segment-wise beam search to select the output that maximizes the utility for diverse preferences.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"Reference: Overfitting\n  In statistics, overfitting is \u0026quot;the production of an analysis that corresponds too closely or exactly to a particular set of data, and may therefore fail to fit additional data or predict future observations reliably\u0026quot;. An overfitted model is a statistical model that contains more parameters than can be justified by the data. The essence of overfitting is to have unknowingly extracted some of the residual variation (i.e., the noise) as if that variation represented underlying model structure. Underfitting occurs when a statistical model cannot adequately capture the underlying structure of the data. An under-fitted model is a model where some parameters or terms that would appear in a correctly specified model are\nModel prediction: [Relevant]Overfitting occurs when a model has too many parameters relative to the amount of data it has been trained on, leading it to memorize the training data too closely and perform poorly on new, unseen data.[Fully supported][Utility:5]\u0026lt;/s\u0026gt;\n\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003eReference: Overfitting\n  In statistics, overfitting is \"the production of an analysis that corresponds too closely or exactly to a particular set of data, and may therefore fail to fit additional data or predict future observations reliably\". An overfitted model is a statistical model that contains more parameters than can be justified by the data. The essence of overfitting is to have unknowingly extracted some of the residual variation (i.e., the noise) as if that variation represented underlying model structure. Underfitting occurs when a statistical model cannot adequately capture the underlying structure of the data. An under-fitted model is a model where some parameters or terms that would appear in a correctly specified model are\nModel prediction: [Relevant]Overfitting occurs when a model has too many parameters relative to the amount of data it has been trained on, leading it to memorize the training data too closely and perform poorly on new, unseen data.[Fully supported][Utility:5]\u0026lt;/s\u0026gt;\n\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe retriever system properly retrieves necessary document and generate fully grounded output.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNote that this demo uses a smaller corpus and Self-RAG with the full inference algorithm. For a full evaluation, you either need to set up a retriever or download our retrieved results. Please follow instructions at \u003ca href=\"#instruction\"\u003eInference\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 Self-RAG.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ew_rel\u003c/code\u003e (default 1.0): \u003ccode\u003ew_rel\u003c/code\u003e controls the emphasis on the \u003ccode\u003eisRel\u003c/code\u003e (a critique token on whether retrieved passages are relevant or not) token probability during beam search.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ew_sup\u003c/code\u003e (default 1.0): \u003ccode\u003ew_sup\u003c/code\u003e controls the emphasis on the \u003ccode\u003eisSup\u003c/code\u003e (a critique token on whether the generation is supported by the document or not) token probability during beam search.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ew_use\u003c/code\u003e (default 0.5): \u003ccode\u003ew_use\u003c/code\u003e controls the emphasis on the \u003ccode\u003eisUse\u003c/code\u003e (a critique token on overall quality) token probability during beam search.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ethreshold\u003c/code\u003e (default 0.2): this threshold controls the frequency of adaptive retrieval.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003emax_depth\u003c/code\u003e (default 6): this corresponds to \u003ccode\u003eT\u003c/code\u003e in the paper, which defines the maximum depth of search.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003ebeam_width\u003c/code\u003e (default 2): this controls the size of the beam in the segment-level beam search.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor more details, please refer to the details (Section 3.3) and analysis (Section 5) in our paper.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class="markdown-body entry-content container-lg" itemprop="text"><div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h1 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">SELF-RAG: Learning to Retrieve, Generate and Critique through Self-reflection</h1><a id="user-content-self-rag-learning-to-retrieve-generate-and-critique-through-self-reflection" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: SELF-RAG: Learning to Retrieve, Generate and Critique through Self-reflection" href="#self-rag-learning-to-retrieve-generate-and-critique-through-self-reflection"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">This includes the original implementation of <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.11511" rel="nofollow">SELF-RAG: Learning to Retrieve, Generate and Critique through self-reflection</a> (ICLR 2024, Oral top 1%) by Akari Asai, Zeqiu Wu, Yizhong Wang, Avirup Sil, and Hannaneh Hajishirzi.</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://selfrag.github.io/" rel="nofollow">Website</a> | <a href="https://huggingface.co/selfrag/selfrag_llama2_7b" rel="nofollow">7B Model</a> | <a href="https://huggingface.co/selfrag/selfrag_llama2_13b" rel="nofollow">13B Model</a> | <a href="https://akariasai.github.io/files/adaptive_retrieval_augmented_lm_arxiv.pdf" rel="nofollow">Paper</a> | <a href="https://huggingface.co/datasets/selfrag/selfrag_train_data" rel="nofollow">Training data</a> | <a href="https://twitter.com/AkariAsai/status/1715110277077962937" rel="nofollow">Twitter summary</a> | <a href="#updates">Updates</a></p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Self-RAG</strong> (Figure right) is a new framework to train an arbitrary LM to learn to retrieve, generate, and critique to enhance the factuality and quality of generations, without hurting the versatility of LLMs.</p>
<p dir="auto">Unlike a widely-adopted Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG; Figure left) approach, <strong>Self-RAG</strong> retrieves on demand (e.g., can retrieve multiple times or completely skip retrieval) given diverse queries, and criticize its own generation from multiple fine-grained aspects by predicting <strong>reflection tokens</strong> as an integral part of generation.
We conduct a segment-wise beam search to select the output that maximizes the utility for diverse preferences.</p>
<p dir="auto"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/images/teaser_self_rag_v8.png"><img src="/AkariAsai/self-rag/raw/main/images/teaser_self_rag_v8.png" alt="" style="max-width: 100%;"></a></p>
<p dir="auto">If you find our code, data, models, or the paper useful, please cite the paper:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@inproceedings{

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Installation</h2><a id="user-content-installation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Installation" href="#installation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Install dependent Python libraries by running the command below.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="pip install -r requirements.txt"><pre class="notranslate"><code>pip install -r requirements.txt
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Please use the latest version of <code>vllm</code>, as the older version may not enable you to set <code>skip_special_tokens</code> via <code>SamplingParam</code>, which is added by (<a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/893" data-hovercard-type="issue" data-hovercard-url="/vllm-project/vllm/issues/893/hovercard">this PR</a>).</p>
<p dir="auto">You can also create a conda environment by running the command below.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="conda env create -f environment.yml"><pre class="notranslate"><code>conda env create -f environment.yml
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Quick start</h2><a id="user-content-quick-start" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Quick start" href="#quick-start"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can download Self-RAG from HuggingFace Hub. For inference, we recommend using <a href="https://vllm.readthedocs.io/en/latest/" rel="nofollow">vllm</a> as it significantly speeds up inferences.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from vllm import LLM, SamplingParams

model = LLM(&quot;selfrag/selfrag_llama2_7b&quot;, download_dir=&quot;/gscratch/h2lab/akari/model_cache&quot;, dtype=&quot;half&quot;)
sampling_params = SamplingParams(temperature=0.0, top_p=1.0, max_tokens=100, skip_special_tokens=False)

def format_prompt(input, paragraph=None):
  prompt = &quot;### Instruction:\n{0}\n\n### Response:\n&quot;.format(input)
  if paragraph is not None:
    prompt += &quot;[Retrieval]&lt;paragraph&gt;{0}&lt;/paragraph&gt;&quot;.format(paragraph)
  return prompt

query_1 = &quot;Leave odd one out: twitter, instagram, whatsapp.&quot;
query_2 = &quot;Can you tell me the difference between llamas and alpacas?&quot;
queries = [query_1, query_2]

# for a query that doesn't require retrieval
preds = model.generate([format_prompt(query) for query in queries], sampling_params)
for pred in preds:
  print(&quot;Model prediction: {0}&quot;.format(pred.outputs[0].text))"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">vllm</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-c1">LLM</span>, <span class="pl-v">SamplingParams</span>

<span class="pl-s1">model</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">LLM</span>(<span class="pl-s">"selfrag/selfrag_llama2_7b"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">download_dir</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"/gscratch/h2lab/akari/model_cache"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">dtype</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"half"</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">sampling_params</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">SamplingParams</span>(<span class="pl-s1">temperature</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0.0</span>, <span class="pl-s1">top_p</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1.0</span>, <span class="pl-s1">max_tokens</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">100</span>, <span class="pl-s1">skip_special_tokens</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">False</span>)

<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">format_prompt</span>(<span class="pl-s1">input</span>, <span class="pl-s1">paragraph</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">None</span>):
  <span class="pl-s1">prompt</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">"### Instruction:<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>{0}<span class="pl-cce">\n</span><span class="pl-cce">\n</span>### Response:<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>.<span class="pl-c1">format</span>(<span class="pl-s1">input</span>)
  <span class="pl-k">if</span> <span class="pl-s1">paragraph</span> <span class="pl-c1"><span class="pl-c1">is</span> <span class="pl-c1">not</span></span> <span class="pl-c1">None</span>:
    <span class="pl-s1">prompt</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"[Retrieval]&lt;paragraph&gt;{0}&lt;/paragraph&gt;"</span>.<span class="pl-c1">format</span>(<span class="pl-s1">paragraph</span>)
  <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">prompt</span>

<span class="pl-s1">query_1</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Leave odd one out: twitter, instagram, whatsapp."</span>
<span class="pl-s1">query_2</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Can you tell me the difference between llamas and alpacas?"</span>
<span class="pl-s1">queries</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [<span class="pl-s1">query_1</span>, <span class="pl-s1">query_2</span>]

<span class="pl-c"># for a query that doesn't require retrieval</span>
<span class="pl-s1">preds</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">model</span>.<span class="pl-c1">generate</span>([<span class="pl-en">format_prompt</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query</span>) <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">query</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-s1">queries</span>], <span class="pl-s1">sampling_params</span>)
<span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">pred</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-s1">preds</span>:
  <span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">"Model prediction: {0}"</span>.<span class="pl-c1">format</span>(<span class="pl-s1">pred</span>.<span class="pl-c1">outputs</span>[<span class="pl-c1">0</span>].<span class="pl-c1">text</span>))</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Output:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-adblock notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Model prediction: Twitter, Instagram, and WhatsApp are all social media platforms. [No Retrieval]WhatsApp is the odd one out because it is a messaging app, while Twitter and # Instagram are primarily used for sharing photos and videos.[Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;
Model prediction: Sure![Retrieval]&lt;paragraph&gt;&lt;paragraph&gt;"><pre>Model prediction: Twitter, Instagram, and WhatsApp are all social media platforms. [No Retrieval]WhatsApp is the odd one out because it is a messaging app, while Twitter and # Instagram are primarily used for sharing photos and videos.[Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;
Model prediction: Sure![Retrieval]&lt;paragraph&gt;&lt;paragraph&gt;</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">As you can see, Self-RAG starts generating responses without retrieval in the first query when it does not require retrieval. On the other hand, Self-RAG output <code>[Retrieve]</code> tokens for the second, as this question requires more fine-grained factual grounding.</p>
<p dir="auto">For queries that require factual grounding, you can insert a paragraph. Self-RAG can retrieve and insert paragraphs anytime while generating, and recognizes them as long as they are surrounded by context markup special tokens <code>&lt;paragraph&gt;</code>, <code>&lt;/paragraph&gt;</code>.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# for a query that needs factual grounding
prompt = format_prompt(&quot;Can you tell me the difference between llamas and alpacas?&quot;, &quot;The alpaca (Lama pacos) is a species of South American camelid mammal. It is similar to, and often confused with, the llama. Alpacas are considerably smaller than llamas, and unlike llamas, they were not bred to be working animals, but were bred specifically for their fiber.&quot;)
preds = model.generate([prompt], sampling_params)
print([pred.outputs[0].text for pred in preds])
# ['[Relevant]Alpacas are considerably smaller than llamas, and unlike llamas, they were not bred to be working animals, but were bred specifically for their fiber.[Fully supported][Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;']"><pre class="notranslate"><code># for a query that needs factual grounding
prompt = format_prompt("Can you tell me the difference between llamas and alpacas?", "The alpaca (Lama pacos) is a species of South American camelid mammal. It is similar to, and often confused with, the llama. Alpacas are considerably smaller than llamas, and unlike llamas, they were not bred to be working animals, but were bred specifically for their fiber.")
preds = model.generate([prompt], sampling_params)
print([pred.outputs[0].text for pred in preds])
# ['[Relevant]Alpacas are considerably smaller than llamas, and unlike llamas, they were not bred to be working animals, but were bred specifically for their fiber.[Fully supported][Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;']
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Self-RAG finds the relevant inserted document and generates answers that are fully supported by the evidence.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Run your evaluation using the online retrieval model</h3><a id="user-content-run-your-evaluation-using-the-online-retrieval-model" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Run your evaluation using the online retrieval model" href="#run-your-evaluation-using-the-online-retrieval-model"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can also run retrieval on-demand and use it with Self-RAG. As running retrieval over full English Wikipedia requires large RAM and multiple GPUs, we created a subset of Wikipedia, including intro paragraphs of Wikipedia articles only for demo purposes.</p>
<p dir="auto">First, please download the corpus and embeddings (9GB in total).</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="git clone git@github.com:AkariAsai/self-rag.git
cd retrieval_lm
bash download_demo_corpus.sh"><pre class="notranslate"><code>git clone git@github.com:AkariAsai/self-rag.git
cd retrieval_lm
bash download_demo_corpus.sh
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If the script does not work, you can download the data from <a href="https://drive.google.com/file/d/1IYNAkwawfCDiBL27BlBqGssxFQH9vOux/view?usp=share_link" rel="nofollow">google drive</a> or <a href="https://huggingface.co/datasets/selfrag/selfrag_train_data" rel="nofollow">HF dataset</a>.
Then, you can run the script under <code>retrieval_lm</code>. We tested the script using on 1 RTX 6000 with 24GB and 100G RAM (but should be runnable with much smaller RAM).</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from passage_retrieval import Retriever
retriever = Retriever({})
retriever.setup_retriever_demo(&quot;facebook/contriever-msmarco&quot;, &quot;enwiki_2020_intro_only/enwiki_2020_dec_intro_only.jsonl&quot;, &quot;enwiki_2020_intro_only/enwiki_dec_2020_contriever_intro/*&quot;,  n_docs=5, save_or_load_index=False)
retrieved_documents = retriever.search_document_demo(query_3, 5)
prompts = [format_prompt(query_3, doc[&quot;title&quot;] +&quot;\n&quot;+ doc[&quot;text&quot;]) for doc in retrieved_documents]
preds = model.generate(prompts, sampling_params)
top_doc = retriever.search_document_demo(query_3, 1)[0]
print(&quot;Reference: {0}\nModel prediction: {1}&quot;.format(top_doc[&quot;title&quot;] + &quot;\n&quot; + top_doc[&quot;text&quot;], preds[0].outputs[0].text))"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">passage_retrieval</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Retriever</span>
<span class="pl-s1">retriever</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Retriever</span>({})
<span class="pl-s1">retriever</span>.<span class="pl-c1">setup_retriever_demo</span>(<span class="pl-s">"facebook/contriever-msmarco"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"enwiki_2020_intro_only/enwiki_2020_dec_intro_only.jsonl"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"enwiki_2020_intro_only/enwiki_dec_2020_contriever_intro/*"</span>,  <span class="pl-s1">n_docs</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">5</span>, <span class="pl-s1">save_or_load_index</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">False</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">retrieved_documents</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">retriever</span>.<span class="pl-c1">search_document_demo</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query_3</span>, <span class="pl-c1">5</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">prompts</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [<span class="pl-en">format_prompt</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query_3</span>, <span class="pl-s1">doc</span>[<span class="pl-s">"title"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span><span class="pl-s">"<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span><span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s1">doc</span>[<span class="pl-s">"text"</span>]) <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">doc</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-s1">retrieved_documents</span>]
<span class="pl-s1">preds</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">model</span>.<span class="pl-c1">generate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">prompts</span>, <span class="pl-s1">sampling_params</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">top_doc</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">retriever</span>.<span class="pl-c1">search_document_demo</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query_3</span>, <span class="pl-c1">1</span>)[<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">"Reference: {0}<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>Model prediction: {1}"</span>.<span class="pl-c1">format</span>(<span class="pl-s1">top_doc</span>[<span class="pl-s">"title"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s">"<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s1">top_doc</span>[<span class="pl-s">"text"</span>], <span class="pl-s1">preds</span>[<span class="pl-c1">0</span>].<span class="pl-c1">outputs</span>[<span class="pl-c1">0</span>].<span class="pl-c1">text</span>))</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Output:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-adblock notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Reference: Overfitting
  In statistics, overfitting is &quot;the production of an analysis that corresponds too closely or exactly to a particular set of data, and may therefore fail to fit additional data or predict future observations reliably&quot;. An overfitted model is a statistical model that contains more parameters than can be justified by the data. The essence of overfitting is to have unknowingly extracted some of the residual variation (i.e., the noise) as if that variation represented underlying model structure. Underfitting occurs when a statistical model cannot adequately capture the underlying structure of the data. An under-fitted model is a model where some parameters or terms that would appear in a correctly specified model are
Model prediction: [Relevant]Overfitting occurs when a model has too many parameters relative to the amount of data it has been trained on, leading it to memorize the training data too closely and perform poorly on new, unseen data.[Fully supported][Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;
"><pre>Reference: Overfitting
  In statistics, overfitting is "the production of an analysis that corresponds too closely or exactly to a particular set of data, and may therefore fail to fit additional data or predict future observations reliably". An overfitted model is a statistical model that contains more parameters than can be justified by the data. The essence of overfitting is to have unknowingly extracted some of the residual variation (i.e., the noise) as if that variation represented underlying model structure. Underfitting occurs when a statistical model cannot adequately capture the underlying structure of the data. An under-fitted model is a model where some parameters or terms that would appear in a correctly specified model are
Model prediction: [Relevant]Overfitting occurs when a model has too many parameters relative to the amount of data it has been trained on, leading it to memorize the training data too closely and perform poorly on new, unseen data.[Fully supported][Utility:5]&lt;/s&gt;
</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">The retriever system properly retrieves necessary document and generate fully grounded output.</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Note that this demo uses a smaller corpus and Self-RAG with the full inference algorithm. For a full evaluation, you either need to set up a retriever or download our retrieved results. Please follow instructions at <a href="#instruction">Inference</a>.</strong></p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Retriever Setup</h2><a id="user-content-retriever-setup" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Retriever Setup" href="#retriever-setup"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">By default, we use <a href="https://github.com/facebookresearch/contriever">Contriever</a> as our retrieval component.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Download data</h3><a id="user-content-download-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Download data" href="#download-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Download preprocessed passage data used in DPR.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cd retrieval_lm
wget https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/dpr/wikipedia_split/psgs_w100.tsv.gz"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cd retrieval_lm
wget https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/dpr/wikipedia_split/psgs_w100.tsv.gz
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Then, download the generated passages. We use <a href="https://huggingface.co/facebook/contriever-msmarco" rel="nofollow">Contriever-MSMARCO</a></p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="wget https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/contriever/embeddings/contriever-msmarco/wikipedia_embeddings.tar"><pre class="notranslate"><code>wget https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/contriever/embeddings/contriever-msmarco/wikipedia_embeddings.tar
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Run retriever</h3><a id="user-content-run-retriever" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Run retriever" href="#run-retriever"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can run passage retrieval by running the command below.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cd retrieval_lm
python passage_retrieval.py \
    --model_name_or_path facebook/contriever-msmarco --passages psgs_w100.tsv \
    --passages_embeddings &quot;wikipedia_embeddings/*&quot; \
    --data YOUR_INPUT_FILE  \
    --output_dir YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE \
    --n_docs 20"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cd retrieval_lm
python passage_retrieval.py \
    --model_name_or_path facebook/contriever-msmarco --passages psgs_w100.tsv \
    --passages_embeddings "wikipedia_embeddings/*" \
    --data YOUR_INPUT_FILE  \
    --output_dir YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE \
    --n_docs 20
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Your input file should be either a <code>json</code> or <code>jsonl</code>. Each instance must contain either <code>question</code> or <code>instruction</code>, which will be used as a query during retrieval.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Generate embeddings for your own data</h3><a id="user-content-generate-embeddings-for-your-own-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Generate embeddings for your own data" href="#generate-embeddings-for-your-own-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">You can generate embeddings for your own data by running the following command. (The script is adapted from the Contriever repository.) Note that generating embeddings from a large-scale corpus (&gt;10M docs) can take time, and we recommend running it on multiple GPUs.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cd retrieval_lm
for i in {0..3}; do
  export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=${i}
  python generate_passage_embeddings.py  --model_name_or_path facebook/contriever-msmarco \
  --output_dir YOUR_OUTPUT_DIR \
  --passages YOUR_PASSAGE_DATA --shard_id ${i}  --num_shards 4 &gt; ./log/nohup.my_embeddings.${i} 2&gt;&amp;1 &amp;"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cd retrieval_lm
for i in {0..3}; do
  export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=${i}
  python generate_passage_embeddings.py  --model_name_or_path facebook/contriever-msmarco \
  --output_dir YOUR_OUTPUT_DIR \
  --passages YOUR_PASSAGE_DATA --shard_id ${i}  --num_shards 4 &gt; ./log/nohup.my_embeddings.${i} 2&gt;&amp;1 &amp;
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Training</h2><a id="user-content-training" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Training" href="#training"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Self-RAG</strong> trains two models, <em>Critic</em> and <em>Generator</em>, both of which expand token vocabularies with reflection tokens and are trained with the standard next token prediction objective.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="#collect-reflection-tokens">Step 1: Critic Data Creation</a>: Generating Critic training data with GPT4.</li>
<li><a href="#critic-training">Step 2: Critic Training</a>:  Training a Critic with new special tokens.</li>
<li><a href="#generator-data-creation">Step 3: Generator Data Creation</a>: Generating Generator training data using Critic and Retriever.</li>
<li><a href="#generator-training">Step 4: Generator Training</a>: Training a Generator with new special tokens.</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">Alternatively, you can download our training data consisting of 150K instances <a href="https://drive.google.com/file/d/10G_FozUV4u27EX0NjwVe-3YMUMeTwuLk/view?usp=share_link" rel="nofollow">here</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Collect reflection tokens</h3><a id="user-content-collect-reflection-tokens" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Collect reflection tokens" href="#collect-reflection-tokens"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We collect training data from GPT-4. The scripts to call GPT-4 for each special token type are available at <a href="/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/data_creation/critic">data_creation/critic</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Alternatively, you can download our training data at <a href="https://drive.google.com/file/d/1IN1XcIOYtRIGWITJ4LKRgfITT-uUwk_W/view?usp=share_link" rel="nofollow">here</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Critic training</h3><a id="user-content-critic-training" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Critic training" href="#critic-training"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Once you create or download training data, run the command below to fine-tune Llama2-7B on critic training.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cd data_creation
torchrun --nproc_per_node=2 \
  --master_port=2568 train_special_tokens.py \
  --model_name_or_path meta-llama/Llama-2-7b-hf \
  --data_path PATH_TO_TRAIN_DATA_FILE \
  --bf16  True \
  --output_dir PATH_TO_CRITIC_MODEL \
  --num_train_epochs 3  \

  --warmup_ratio 0.01 \

  --fsdp &quot;full_shard auto_wrap&quot;"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cd data_creation

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Generator Data Creation</h3><a id="user-content-generator-data-creation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Generator Data Creation" href="#generator-data-creation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The code to create Generator training data is under <a href="/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/data_creation/generator">generator_data_creation</a>. See the instructions at <a href="/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/data_creation/generator/README.md">README.md</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Alternatively, you can download our training data at <a href="https://huggingface.co/datasets/selfrag/selfrag_train_data/tree/main" rel="nofollow">HuggingFace dataset</a> or <a href="https://drive.google.com/file/d/10G_FozUV4u27EX0NjwVe-3YMUMeTwuLk/view?usp=share_link" rel="nofollow">here</a></p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Generator Training</h3><a id="user-content-generator-training" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Generator Training" href="#generator-training"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For generator training, we use DeepSpeed to make training more efficient. You can run training by running the script below, after setting the training data path.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cd retrieval_lm
bash script_finetune_7b.sh"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cd retrieval_lm
bash script_finetune_7b.sh
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">For 13B model training, use <code>training_13b</code>. We use 8 A100 with 40 GRAM for 7B model training and 4 a100 with 80 GB GRAM for 13B training. 7B should fit 1-2 A100 although training can be slow.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Inference</h2><a id="user-content-inference" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Inference" href="#inference"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For the task evaluation conducted in the paper, please download the data <a href="https://drive.google.com/file/d/1TLKhWjez63H4uBtgCxyoyJsZi-IMgnDb/view?usp=share_link" rel="nofollow">here</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Each file already comes with retrieved documents, so if you don't want to run a retriever as a part of inference, you can simply load the retrieved docs at <code>contexts</code>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Below, we describe Self-RAG and baselines.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="#shot_form">Short-form</a>: run evaluation for short-form generation.</li>
<li><a href="#long_form">Long-form</a>: run evaluations for long-form generations.</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Short-form (PubHealth, ARC-Challenge, TriviaQA, PopQA)</h3><a id="user-content-short-form-pubhealth-arc-challenge-triviaqa-popqa" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Short-form (PubHealth, ARC-Challenge, TriviaQA, PopQA)" href="#short-form-pubhealth-arc-challenge-triviaqa-popqa"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">As we typically retrieve only once for a short-form generation task, we provide an easy-to-run evaluation script that leverages pre-given documents retrieved by Contriever offline. See the individual command below.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Question Answering</h4><a id="user-content-question-answering" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Question Answering" href="#question-answering"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python run_short_form.py \
--model_name selfrag/selfrag_llama2_7b \
--input_file eval_data/popqa_longtail_w_gs.jsonl \
--mode MODE --max_new_tokens 100 \
--threshold 0.2 \
--output_file YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE \
--metric match --ndocs 10 --use_groundness --use_utility --use_seqscore \
--dtype half"><pre class="notranslate"><code>python run_short_form.py \
--model_name selfrag/selfrag_llama2_7b \
--input_file eval_data/popqa_longtail_w_gs.jsonl \
--mode MODE --max_new_tokens 100 \
--threshold 0.2 \
--output_file YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE \
--metric match --ndocs 10 --use_groundness --use_utility --use_seqscore \
--dtype half
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto"><code>mode</code> specifies the inference time mode among <code>['adaptive_retrieval', 'no_retrieval', 'always_retrieve']</code>.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code>adaptive_retrieval</code> retrieves given the <code>threshold</code> or Self-RAG prediction</li>
<li><code>no_retrieval</code> disables retrieval at inference time</li>
<li><code>always_retrieve</code> always retrieves.</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">For 13B, you may have an OOM issue if you use a single GPU with 24 GRAM. You can run inference on multiple GPUs by setting <code>--world_size</code>.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Long-form (ASQA, FactScore)</h3><a id="user-content-long-form-asqa-factscore" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Long-form (ASQA, FactScore)" href="#long-form-asqa-factscore"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For long-form QA, you can either run evaluations with a retrieval model or with pre-given passages.
Currently, we are working on reducing run-time memory requirements (DPR / Contriever with the whole English Wikipedia Embeddings requires 100 GB RAM) speeding up for long-form generations, and releasing the inference code using a small set of initial retrieved documents first (~20).</p>
<p dir="auto"><em>Note: Our current implementation is specifically designed for evaluations of target task datasets. We are planning to update our code base to make the interface more simple and easier to use. We will announce it when we release another version.</em></p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Run inference using pre-retrieved passages</h4><a id="user-content-run-inference-using-pre-retrieved-passages" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Run inference using pre-retrieved passages" href="#run-inference-using-pre-retrieved-passages"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For ASQA, please run the following command,</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h5 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Key parameters for long-form generations</h5><a id="user-content-key-parameters-for-long-form-generations" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Key parameters for long-form generations" href="#key-parameters-for-long-form-generations"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">There are several key parameters related to the inference of Self-RAG.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code>w_rel</code> (default 1.0): <code>w_rel</code> controls the emphasis on the <code>isRel</code> (a critique token on whether retrieved passages are relevant or not) token probability during beam search.</li>
<li><code>w_sup</code> (default 1.0): <code>w_sup</code> controls the emphasis on the <code>isSup</code> (a critique token on whether the generation is supported by the document or not) token probability during beam search.</li>
<li><code>w_use</code> (default 0.5): <code>w_use</code> controls the emphasis on the <code>isUse</code> (a critique token on overall quality) token probability during beam search.</li>
<li><code>threshold</code> (default 0.2): this threshold controls the frequency of adaptive retrieval.</li>
<li><code>max_depth</code> (default 6): this corresponds to <code>T</code> in the paper, which defines the maximum depth of search.</li>
<li><code>beam_width</code> (default 2): this controls the size of the beam in the segment-level beam search.</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">For more details, please refer to the details (Section 3.3) and analysis (Section 5) in our paper.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Run evaluation</h4><a id="user-content-run-evaluation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Run evaluation" href="#run-evaluation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For long-form evaluations, set up external libraries or repositories to run evaluations.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code>factscore==v0.1.5</code> (bio)
Please follow the instructions at the <a href="https://github.com/shmsw25/FActScore">FactScore</a> official repository to set up your environment.</li>
</ul>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python -m factscore.factscorer --data_path YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE  --model_name retrieval+ChatGPT --cache_dir YOUR_CACHE_DIR --openai_key YOUR_OPEN_AI_KEY --verbose"><pre class="notranslate"><code>python -m factscore.factscorer --data_path YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE  --model_name retrieval+ChatGPT --cache_dir YOUR_CACHE_DIR --openai_key YOUR_OPEN_AI_KEY --verbose
</code></pre></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="https://github.com/princeton-nlp/ALCE">ALCE/ASQA</a></li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">ALCE provides a comprehensive evaluation using multiple different metrics for long-form QA. For your first evaluation, install the ALCE repo and download the data.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="git clone https://github.com/princeton-nlp/ALCE.git
python3 -m alce_env
cd ALCE
bash download_data.sh"><pre class="notranslate"><code>git clone https://github.com/princeton-nlp/ALCE.git
python3 -m alce_env
cd ALCE
bash download_data.sh
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">For ASQA, you can run evaluations as follows. Note that ASQA evaluations require T5-XXL (11B)-based NLI module.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="python eval.py --f YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE --citations --qa --mauve"><pre class="notranslate"><code>python eval.py --f YOUR_OUTPUT_FILE --citations --qa --mauve
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Baselines</h2><a id="user-content-baselines" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Baselines" href="#baselines"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Code to rerun the baselines is available at <a href="https://github.com/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/retrieval_lm/run_baseline_lm.py">run_baseline_lm.py</a>.
To run the retrieval-augmented baselines, make sure to download the task input files with retrieved passages.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">FAQ</h2><a id="user-content-faq" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: FAQ" href="#faq"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Q1: How can I train a new pre-trained LM using Self-RAG scheme?</strong> -- If you are using hugging face transformers, you can simply change the <code>model_name_or_path</code> and <code>tokenizer_name</code> in our training script, <a href="https://github.com/AkariAsai/self-rag/blob/main/retrieval_lm/script_finetune_7b.sh">script_finetune_7b.sh</a>. If you want to use your own fine-tuning script, please make sure to add the special tokens and mask out the paragraph context, as discussed in <a href="https://github.com/AkariAsai/self-rag/issues/12" data-hovercard-type="issue" data-hovercard-url="/AkariAsai/self-rag/issues/12/hovercard">this issue</a></p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Q2: Are you planning to release Mistral-7B-based Self-RAG?</strong> -- Right now I have limited bandwidth to do so, but there is a community-trained version of Self-RAG <a href="https://huggingface.co/SciPhi/SciPhi-Self-RAG-Mistral-7B-32k" rel="nofollow">SciPhi-Self-RAG-Mistral-7B-32k</a> on top of Mistral-7B. We will announce if we can train Self-RAG on Mistral-7B and release the checkpoint.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Contact</h2><a id="user-content-contact" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Contact" href="#contact"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">If you have questions, please open an issue mentioning @AkariAsai or send an email to akari[at]cs.washington.edu.</p>
